# Operation SOS Content Implementation Plan (sub-plan 03)

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Deliver every authored document under `playbooks/` (20 scenarios, 17 modules, 23 medical quick cards, 12 pages), the three content manifests `manifest/core.json`, `manifest/extended.json`, `manifest/overlays.json`, and the hand-authored `tools/map-styles/data/nuclear-sites.geojson`, with `sos validate-playbooks` printing `OK 72 documents` and the manifest tests green.

**Architecture:** Content is data, not code: JSON manifests describe every downloadable item (Kiwix ZIMs resolved by OPDS name, `url` PDFs with sha256, `build` artefacts produced on the owner's PC), and Markdown files with YAML front matter carry the playbooks. Plan 01's `sos.manifest` and `sos.content` modules are the only executables involved; this plan adds two pytest files that pin the content decisions (ids, weights, suggest flags, overlay ids, citation coverage, card screen rule) on top of the generic schema test and the validator.

**Tech Stack:** JSON (manifest/schema.json from plan 01), Markdown + YAML front matter (python-frontmatter, markdown-it-py via plan 01), GeoJSON, pytest, the `sos` CLI.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-operation-sos-design.md` (sections 2, 7, 9, 10, 14, 15). Overview: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-00-overview.md` (locked layout, contracts and link scheme). Research: `docs/research/2026-09-03-research-brief.md`.

**Deviations** (overview wins over the spec; each noted here once):
- `manifest/overlays.json` overlay objects carry `id, kind, layer_id?, default_on, coverage, color, icon` only; the spec's `scenarios_on` is omitted because the overview derives it at index time from playbook front matter.
- Pages carry `category: comms | reference | plan | about` (overview addition to the spec's `id, title, icon, order, summary`).
- The spec's example `reader_home: "A/Main_Page"` is not the path convention of current ZIMs (verified on the sample library: Wikipedia articles are flat paths such as `Potassium_iodide`, the Wikipedia 100 main page is `index`, zimit ZIMs use `www.nhs.uk/medicines/`, zimgit ZIMs use `home`, iFixit uses `home/home`, Stack Exchange ZIMs use `questions`). `reader_home` is set only where verified.
- The Kiwix OPDS `?name=` filter matches the bare catalogue name (`wikipedia_en_all`) and returns every flavour; `?name=wikipedia_en_all_maxi` returns nothing (verified 2026-09-03). Item ids keep the spec's flavoured form (`wikipedia_en_all_maxi`) and `source.name` is the bare OPDS name; the flavour is the id's trailing `_maxi | _nopic | _mini` token. Plan 01's resolver must pick the entry whose `<flavour>` equals that token (see "Decisions for other plans" at the end).
- Spec 7 lists "TED-Ed" for the extended tier; the Kiwix catalogue has no TED-Ed ZIM as of 2026-09-03 (only `ted_mul_<topic>` multi-language video packs), so it is left out of `extended.json`.
- Spec 7 lists Ofcom PMR446 and amateur licence documents as core items. Ofcom moved its document URLs in 2024 and none of the historical paths resolve; both are `build` items with `tool: "manual"` (owner downloads them from ofcom.org.uk) rather than `url` items with an invented address.

## Global Constraints

- Python 3.12 or newer (the Pi runs 3.13); Node 22 or newer; pnpm for the frontend.
- No Docker anywhere. No runtime dependency on the internet. No CDN references in any HTML, CSS or JS.
- Manifest invariant: every `zim` item's `dest` is `zim/<id>.zim`, so the Kiwix book name in URLs equals the manifest item id.
- All authored content is British English. Emergency numbers are 999, 111, 105, 0345 988 1188 (Floodline). Drug names are UK names (paracetamol, adrenaline, salbutamol, glyceryl trinitrate).
- Never name the product "NOMAD". The product name is "Operation SOS"; the short name in UI copy is "SOS".
- Every commit passes `make test`.
- Licence is informational (`licence` field) and never gates behaviour.
- `search_weight` (default 1.0): 1.4 on every uk-official item and the NHS ZIMs, 1.2 on other medical items. `suggest` (default false): true on Wikipedia, WikiMed, the NHS ZIMs and iFixit.
- `as_at` is `YYYY-MM-DD`, or `YYYY-MM` when only a month is known, in manifests and front matter alike.
- A `sources` entry must have `doc:` or `kiwix:` if the source is in the manifest; `url` is provenance only and is never rendered as a link.
- Scenario bodies contain exactly, in order: `## Right now`, `## First 72 hours`, `## First month`, `## Long term`, `## UK specifics`, `## Checklist`, `## Go deeper`; all non-empty. `## Checklist` contains only `- [ ] text` or `- [ ] text {#id}` lines. `{{module:<slug>}}` on its own line includes a declared module; every declared module is included exactly once and every include is declared.
- Link scheme: `kiwix:<id>/<path>`, `doc:<id>`, `doc:<id>#page=<n>`, `map:?overlay=x`, `playbook:<slug>`, `module:<slug>`, `card:<slug>`, `page:<slug>`.
- Medical quick cards: the title and first three steps fit without scrolling at 853x480 CSS px and at 360px phone width.
- Every factual claim that matters (a dose, a distance, a time, a law) carries an inline citation to a manifest item and page.
- Every scenario has at least one citation in Right now, First 72 hours and UK specifics (spec milestone 4).

---

## What this plan consumes from plan 01 (must exist before Task 1)

| Thing | Exact interface |
|---|---|
| `manifest/schema.json` | JSON Schema for `{"items": [...]}`; item fields and enums as in the overview |
| `sos.manifest` | `load_manifests(dir: Path) -> list[Item]`, `validate_manifests(dir: Path) -> list[str]` (errors, empty if valid); both tolerate a directory holding only some of the four files (the dev manifest dir has only `core.json`) |
| `sos.content` | `parse_document(path: Path) -> Document` with `id, title, icon, order, summary, kind, modules, overlays, reviewed, sources, sections: list[tuple[str, str, str]]` (`(id, title, markdown)`), `checklist: list[tuple[str, str]]` (`(item_id, text)`), `category`; `validate_tree(playbooks_dir: Path, manifest_items: list[Item], overlay_ids: list[str]) -> list[str]` |
| `sos validate-playbooks [--deep]` | exit 1 with errors printed, else `OK <n> documents` |
| `playbooks/schema.json`, `playbooks/README.md` | front matter schema and authoring rules (this plan appends a "Conventions" section to the README in Task 3) |
| `make test` | runs pytest, vitest, `sos validate-playbooks` and the manifest schema test |

Tests in this plan import from `sos` and run from `api/`: `cd api && pytest tests/<file> -q`. Paths to the repo are computed as `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]`.

## File map (everything this plan creates)

| Path | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `manifest/core.json` | 115 core-tier items (uk-official, medical, survival, reference, practical, ai) |
| `manifest/extended.json` | extended-tier items: Gutenberg, Stack Overflow, 145 Stack Exchange sites, Khan Academy, LibreTexts, OpenStax PDFs, Survivor Library, video crawls, Welsh Wikipedia, media folders |
| `manifest/overlays.json` | the 10 map overlays with their `overlay` objects |
| `tools/map-styles/data/nuclear-sites.geojson` | hand-authored nuclear-sites overlay source (37 features) |
| `tools/zimit/prepare_uk.txt`, `tools/zimit/govuk_resilience.txt`, `tools/zimit/legislation_uk.txt` | seed URL lists for the three self-built ZIMs. Reason for a path outside the overview layout: `build` items need a reproducible input list and the manifest `source` object has no field for it; the overview's `tools/` directory already holds build inputs (`tools/map-styles`, `tools/eval`) |
| `playbooks/modules/<slug>.md` (17) | shared modules |
| `playbooks/cards/<slug>.md` (23) | medical quick cards |
| `playbooks/pages/<slug>.md` (12) | comms, reference, plan and about pages |
| `playbooks/scenarios/<slug>.md` (20) | scenario playbooks |
| `playbooks/README.md` | append "Conventions" (Task 3) |
| `api/tests/test_manifest_content.py` | content decisions for the manifests and the GeoJSON |
| `api/tests/test_playbooks_content.py` | content decisions for playbooks beyond the validator |

## Conventions used by every task

**Icon vocabulary.** `icon:` values are lowercase words from this list; plan 02's `icons.tsx` maps each one and falls back to `book` for unknown names. Modules: `water food home medical toilet bolt radio shield compass people heart radiation exit car tools seedling cow`. Scenarios: `radiation atom virus bolt sun zap laptop flag fire coins truck waves snowflake thermometer mountain flask wheat cloud alert hammer`. Cards: `heart child blood lungs fire snowflake thermometer bone radiation flask baby water bandage alert waves brain syringe cloud face heart bed sugar lungs`. Pages: `radio antenna phone plug clipboard water bolt sun shield leaf bug info`.

**Module body headings** (free per spec; fixed here so modules read alike): `## Key facts`, `## What to do`, `## UK specifics`, `## Go deeper`. Modules contain no `- [ ]` task lines (checklist state is per playbook; keeping task lines out of modules avoids id collisions between the scenarios that include them).

**Card body headings**, in this order and no others: `## When to use`, `## Steps`, `## Warnings`, `## Stop or escalate`, `## Source`. `## Steps` is an ordered list; steps 1 to 3 are at most 70 characters each and `## When to use` is at most 110 characters, which is what makes the screen rule hold at 853x480 and 360px (title 40px + when-to-use two lines + three steps of at most three lines at 22px body). `## Warnings` is one or more lines starting with `**Warning:**` (word plus the frontend's symbol, never colour alone).

**Page bodies** are free-form Markdown with `##` headings.

**Scenario sections.** `{{module:<slug>}}` lines go in the section where the module is first needed (usually `## First 72 hours` or `## First month`), each declared module exactly once. Checklist items always carry an explicit `{#id}` (kebab-case, 3 to 40 characters), so rewording never resets state.

**Citations.** Inline, immediately after the claim: `([Prepare](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/))`, `([NRR 2025, p. 45](doc:nrr-2025#page=45))`. Page numbers are PDF page indexes (the page the viewer opens with `#page=n`), verified with pypdf on 2026-09-03 and listed per task.

**`as_at` when only the year of a public-domain edition is known** (NWSS 1987, Emergency War Surgery 2018, Survival and Austere Medicine 2017, FM manuals with a known month use it): `YYYY-01`, and the `description` names the edition. The fact-check for those items is "confirm the publication month from the title page and correct `as_at`".

**`build` items.** `tool` is `zimit` (seed list in `tools/zimit/<id>.txt`), `build-nhs` (plan 01's crawler), `build-maps` (plan 04) or `manual` (the owner downloads the artefact by hand from the site named in `description`; `sos sync` prints copy instructions for every `build` item). `artifact` is the file name `sos sync` expects under `SOS_CORE` or `SOS_EXT` at `dest`.

**Zimit path convention** (verified on `nhs.uk_en_medicines_2025-12.zim`): the article path is the crawled URL without scheme, e.g. `www.gov.uk/buying-carrying-knives`, `prepare.campaign.gov.uk/coping-with-trauma/`, `www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1988/33/section/139`. Wikipedia ZIM paths are the article title with underscores (`Acute_radiation_syndrome`). Stack Exchange ZIM paths are `questions/<id>/<slug>` with main page `questions`. zimgit ZIM main page is `home`; iFixit device pages are `Device/<Name>` with main page `home/home`.

**Verified item facts** (2026-09-03): OPDS names, sizes and dates below come from `https://opds.library.kiwix.org/catalog/v2/entries?count=-1`; PDF sizes and sha256 values come from downloading each file; every seed URL returned HTTP 200.

### Task 1: manifest/core.json

**Files:**
- Create: `manifest/core.json`
- Create: `tools/zimit/prepare_uk.txt`, `tools/zimit/govuk_resilience.txt`, `tools/zimit/legislation_uk.txt`
- Test: `api/tests/test_manifest_content.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `sos.manifest.validate_manifests(dir) -> list[str]` (plan 01), `manifest/schema.json`.
- Produces: the core item ids every later task cites in `doc:` / `kiwix:` links and `sources:` entries. The full id list is the `CORE_REQUIRED` set in the test below.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `api/tests/test_manifest_content.py`:

```python
"""Content decisions for the repo manifests (sub-plan 03).

Plan 01's test_manifest.py checks shape against manifest/schema.json.
These tests pin what the manifests say: ids, weights, suggest flags,
source conventions, overlay objects and the nuclear-sites GeoJSON.
"""
import json
import re
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from sos.manifest import validate_manifests

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
MANIFEST_DIR = REPO / "manifest"
ZIMIT_DIR = REPO / "tools" / "zimit"

AS_AT = re.compile(r"^\d{4}-\d{2}(-\d{2})?$")
SCENARIOS = [
    "nuclear-war", "nuclear-accident", "pandemic", "grid-collapse", "solar-storm", "emp",
    "cyber-attack", "invasion", "civil-unrest", "economic-collapse", "supply-chain",
    "storms-flooding", "severe-winter", "heat-drought", "volcanic", "chemical", "famine",
    "impact-winter", "terrorism", "long-rebuild",
]

CORE_REQUIRED = {
    # uk-official
    "prepare_uk", "nrr-2025", "resilience-action-plan-2025", "wales-resilience-framework-2025",
    "awhp-2026", "govuk_resilience", "legislation_uk", "ukhsa-radiation-decontamination",
    "nrpb-stable-iodine", "protect-and-survive-1980", "rsgb-band-plan-2026",
    "ofcom-pmr446-ir2009", "ofcom-amateur-licence-2024",
    "hse-indg231", "hse-indg317", "hse-indg258", "hse-indg370",
    "ad-a", "ad-b1", "ad-c", "ad-d", "ad-e", "ad-f1", "ad-g", "ad-h", "ad-j", "ad-k", "ad-l1",
    "ad-m1", "ad-o", "ad-p", "ad-q", "ad-r1", "ad-s", "ad-t", "ad-reg7",
    # medical
    "nhs_uk", "nhs_medicines", "wikipedia_en_medicine_maxi", "wikem_en_all_maxi",
    "mdwiki_en_all_maxi", "medlineplus.gov_en_all", "zimgit-medicine_en",
    "where-there-is-no-doctor", "where-there-is-no-dentist",
    "scmg-ch01", "scmg-ch02", "scmg-ch03", "scmg-ch04", "scmg-ch05", "scmg-ch06", "scmg-ch07",
    "scmg-ch09", "scmg-ch10", "scmg-ch11", "scmg-ch12", "scmg-annex",
    "who-eml-2025", "irp.fas.org_en_military-medicine", "survival-austere-medicine-2017",
    "emergency-war-surgery-2018", "fm-4-25-11-first-aid", "medicalsciences.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    # survival
    "zimgit-water_en", "zimgit-food-preparation_en", "zimgit-knots_en", "zimgit-post-disaster_en",
    "www.ready.gov_en", "appropedia_en_all", "cd3wdproject.org_en_all", "nwss",
    "fema-nuclear-detonation-2022", "fm-21-76-survival", "fm-3-05-70-survival",
    "urban-prepper_en_all", "trueprepper.com_en_all", "lrnselfreliance_en_all",
    "energypedia_en_all_maxi", "solar.lowtechmagazine.com_mul_all", "based.cooking_en_all",
    "foss.cooking_en_all", "usda-2015_en",
    # reference
    "wikipedia_en_all_maxi", "wikipedia_en-simple_all_maxi", "wiktionary_en_all_nopic",
    "wikibooks_en_all_maxi", "wikivoyage_en_all_maxi", "openstreetmap-wiki_en_all_maxi",
    # practical
    "ifixit_en_all",
    "diy.stackexchange.com_en_all", "electronics.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "gardening.stackexchange.com_en_all", "outdoors.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "mechanics.stackexchange.com_en_all", "woodworking.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "cooking.stackexchange.com_en_all", "homebrew.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "sustainability.stackexchange.com_en_all", "ham.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "bicycles.stackexchange.com_en_all", "biology.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "chemistry.stackexchange.com_en_all", "physics.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "engineering.stackexchange.com_en_all", "earthscience.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "pets.stackexchange.com_en_all",
    "devdocs_en_python", "devdocs_en_bash", "devdocs_en_sqlite", "devdocs_en_html",
    "devdocs_en_css", "devdocs_en_javascript",
    # ai
    "gemma-4-E2B-it-Q4_K_M", "Qwen3.5-2B-Q4_K_M", "gemma-3-1b-it-Q4_K_M",
}


def items(name: str) -> list[dict]:
    return json.loads((MANIFEST_DIR / name).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["items"]


def by_id(name: str) -> dict[str, dict]:
    rows = items(name)
    ids = [r["id"] for r in rows]
    assert len(ids) == len(set(ids)), f"duplicate ids in {name}"
    return {r["id"]: r for r in rows}


def test_repo_manifests_validate():
    assert validate_manifests(MANIFEST_DIR) == []


def test_core_required_ids_present():
    core = by_id("core.json")
    assert sorted(CORE_REQUIRED - core.keys()) == []


def test_core_ids_are_all_expected():
    core = by_id("core.json")
    assert sorted(core.keys() - CORE_REQUIRED) == []


def test_core_tier_and_categories():
    for it in items("core.json"):
        assert it["tier"] == "core", it["id"]
        assert it["category"] in {"uk-official", "medical", "survival", "reference", "practical", "ai"}, it["id"]
        assert set(it["scenarios"]) <= set(SCENARIOS), it["id"]


def test_search_weights():
    for it in items("core.json"):
        w = it.get("search_weight", 1.0)
        if it["category"] == "uk-official" or it["id"] in {"nhs_uk", "nhs_medicines"}:
            assert w == 1.4, it["id"]
        elif it["category"] == "medical":
            assert w == 1.2, it["id"]
        else:
            assert w == 1.0, it["id"]


def test_suggest_flags():
    got = {it["id"] for it in items("core.json") if it.get("suggest")}
    assert got == {"wikipedia_en_all_maxi", "wikipedia_en_medicine_maxi", "nhs_uk", "nhs_medicines", "ifixit_en_all"}


def test_zim_dest_invariant_and_kiwix_names():
    for it in items("core.json"):
        if it["kind"] == "zim":
            assert it["dest"] == f"zim/{it['id']}.zim", it["id"]
        if it["source"]["type"] == "kiwix":
            name = it["source"]["name"]
            assert not name.endswith(("_maxi", "_nopic", "_mini")), it["id"]
            if it["id"] == "nhs_medicines":  # the one spec-fixed id that is not the OPDS name
                assert name == "nhs.uk_en_medicines"
                continue
            head, _, tail = it["id"].rpartition("_")
            assert it["id"] == name or (head == name and tail in {"maxi", "nopic", "mini"}), it["id"]


def test_url_items_are_https_with_sha256():
    for it in items("core.json"):
        if it["source"]["type"] == "url":
            assert it["source"]["url"].startswith("https://"), it["id"]
            assert re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{64}", it["source"].get("sha256", "")), it["id"]
            assert it["size_bytes"] > 0, it["id"]


def test_build_items_have_artifact_and_seed_lists():
    for it in items("core.json"):
        src = it["source"]
        if src["type"] == "build":
            assert src["tool"] in {"zimit", "build-nhs", "manual"}, it["id"]
            assert src["artifact"].endswith((".zim", ".pdf", ".gguf")), it["id"]
            if src["tool"] == "zimit":
                seeds = (ZIMIT_DIR / f"{it['id']}.txt").read_text(encoding="utf-8").split()
                assert seeds and all(s.startswith("https://") for s in seeds), it["id"]


def test_as_at_and_priority():
    for it in items("core.json"):
        assert AS_AT.match(it["as_at"]), it["id"]
        assert isinstance(it["priority"], int) and it["priority"] > 0, it["id"]
    core = by_id("core.json")
    assert core["prepare_uk"]["priority"] == 1
    assert core["nrr-2025"]["priority"] == 2
    assert core["nhs_uk"]["priority"] < core["nhs_medicines"]["priority"] < core["wikipedia_en_all_maxi"]["priority"]


def test_reader_home_only_where_verified():
    core = by_id("core.json")
    assert core["nhs_medicines"]["reader_home"] == "www.nhs.uk/medicines/"
    assert core["nhs_uk"]["reader_home"] == "www.nhs.uk/conditions/"
    assert core["zimgit-water_en"]["reader_home"] == "home"
    assert core["ifixit_en_all"]["reader_home"] == "home/home"
    assert core["homebrew.stackexchange.com_en_all"]["reader_home"] == "questions"
    assert "reader_home" not in core["wikipedia_en_all_maxi"]


def test_core_size_near_target():
    total = sum(it["size_bytes"] for it in items("core.json"))
    assert 150e9 < total < 230e9, total
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `manifest/core.json`**

One item per line inside `"items": [ ... ]` (keep the one-line-per-item layout for diffs; check validity with `python3 -m json.tool manifest/core.json > /dev/null`, never reformat). The `ALL` scenarios list on `nrr-2025` is the full 20-slug list written out. Descriptions are one sentence. Verified sizes, dates, URLs and sha256 values are used verbatim.

Notes for the executor:
- `size_bytes` for `build` items (`prepare_uk` 147.6 MB from the Zimfarm build log in the brief, `govuk_resilience`, `legislation_uk`, `nhs_uk`, the Ofcom PDFs, the Hesperian PDFs, the Gemma 3 model) are estimates; every other size is the measured byte count. The two model files that exist on the PC (`~/sos-content/models`) are the measured sizes.
- Item count is 115. The size sum is about 188 GB, inside the 150 to 230 GB window the test allows around the spec's "about 200GB" target.

- [ ] **Step 5: Run the tests to verify they pass**

Run: `python3 -m json.tool manifest/core.json > /dev/null && cd api && pytest tests/test_manifest_content.py -q`
Expected: `12 passed` (the extended and overlay tests are added in Task 2).

- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**

```bash
git add manifest/core.json tools/zimit/prepare_uk.txt tools/zimit/govuk_resilience.txt tools/zimit/legislation_uk.txt api/tests/test_manifest_content.py
git commit -m "feat(content): add core manifest with verified sources and zimit seed lists"
```

**Fact-check checklist (reviewer, Task 1):**
- [ ] Every `url` item: open the URL in a browser; the file downloads and `sha256sum` matches the manifest.
- [ ] `nrr-2025` is the January 2025 edition (cover says "National Risk Register 2025 edition").
- [ ] `fema-nuclear-detonation-2022` cover says "Third Edition" (page 103 of the PDF references both editions; confirm on the cover).
- [ ] `who-eml-2025` first page says "24th list (2025)".
- [ ] `awhp-2026` cover says "2026 to 2027".
- [ ] Approved Document `as_at` months match the "came into effect" date printed inside each PDF; correct any that differ.

### Task 2: manifest/extended.json, manifest/overlays.json and the nuclear-sites GeoJSON

**Files:**
- Create: `manifest/extended.json`
- Create: `manifest/overlays.json`
- Create: `tools/map-styles/data/nuclear-sites.geojson`
- Modify: `api/tests/test_manifest_content.py` (append the tests below)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `sos.manifest.validate_manifests(dir)`; the `overlay` object shape from the overview (`id, kind, default_on, coverage, color, icon`; `layer_id` only for `style-layer`).
- Produces: overlay ids `footpaths access-land flood-zones health fuel water rail nuclear-sites chemical-sites airports-military` used by every scenario's `overlays:` front matter and `map:` links (Tasks 6 and 7), and the `overlay_ids()` helper used by `test_playbooks_content.py`. Plan 04's `build-maps` reads `tools/map-styles/data/nuclear-sites.geojson` and writes `maps/overlays/nuclear-sites.geojson`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Append the failing tests**

Append to `api/tests/test_manifest_content.py`:

```python
OVERLAY_IDS = [
    "footpaths", "access-land", "flood-zones", "health", "fuel", "water", "rail",
    "nuclear-sites", "chemical-sites", "airports-military",
]
REGIONS = ["england", "wales", "scotland", "ni", "roi", "iom", "ci"]
EXTENDED_REQUIRED = {
    "gutenberg_en_all", "stackoverflow.com_en_all", "khanacademy_en_all", "survivorlibrary.com_en_all",
    "wikipedia_cy_all_maxi", "libretexts.org_en_med", "libretexts.org_en_bio", "openstax-biology-2e",
    "openstax-anatomy-physiology-2e", "s2underground_en_all", "canadian-prepper_en_winterprepping",
    "media-films", "media-music", "media-audiobooks", "owner-books",
}


def test_extended_tier_and_required_ids():
    ext = by_id("extended.json")
    assert sorted(EXTENDED_REQUIRED - ext.keys()) == []
    for it in ext.values():
        assert it["tier"] == "extended", it["id"]
        assert it["category"] in {"practical", "education", "books", "media", "reference"}, it["id"]
        assert AS_AT.match(it["as_at"]), it["id"]
        if it["kind"] == "zim":
            assert it["dest"] == f"zim/{it['id']}.zim", it["id"]
        if it["kind"] == "dir":
            assert it["source"] == {"type": "build", "tool": "manual", "artifact": it["dest"]}, it["id"]
        if it["source"]["type"] == "url":
            assert it["source"]["url"].startswith("https://") and it["size_bytes"] > 0, it["id"]


def test_extended_holds_every_stack_exchange_site_not_in_core():
    core = by_id("core.json")
    ext = by_id("extended.json")
    se_ext = [i for i in ext if i.endswith(".stackexchange.com_en_all")]
    assert len(se_ext) == 140
    assert not (set(se_ext) & set(core))
    for i in se_ext:
        assert ext[i]["reader_home"] == "questions"


def test_overlay_ids_and_objects():
    rows = items("overlays.json")
    assert [r["id"] for r in rows] == OVERLAY_IDS
    for r in rows:
        assert r["tier"] == "core" and r["category"] == "maps", r["id"]
        assert r["kind"] in {"pmtiles", "geojson"}, r["id"]
        assert r["dest"] == f"maps/overlays/{r['id']}.{r['kind']}", r["id"]
        assert r["source"] == {"type": "build", "tool": "build-maps", "artifact": f"overlays/{r['id']}.{r['kind']}"}, r["id"]
        ov = r["overlay"]
        assert ov["id"] == r["id"] and ov["kind"] == r["kind"], r["id"]
        assert isinstance(ov["default_on"], bool), r["id"]
        assert ov["coverage"] and set(ov["coverage"]) <= set(REGIONS), r["id"]
        assert re.fullmatch(r"#[0-9a-f]{6}", ov["color"]), r["id"]
        assert ov["icon"] is None or isinstance(ov["icon"], str), r["id"]
        assert "layer_id" not in ov, r["id"]
    ov = {r["id"]: r["overlay"] for r in rows}
    assert [i for i in OVERLAY_IDS if ov[i]["default_on"]] == ["footpaths"]
    assert ov["footpaths"]["coverage"] == REGIONS
    assert ov["access-land"]["coverage"] == ["england", "wales"]
    assert ov["flood-zones"]["coverage"] == ["england", "wales", "scotland", "ni"]
    for i in ("health", "fuel", "water", "rail", "nuclear-sites", "chemical-sites", "airports-military"):
        assert ov[i]["coverage"] == REGIONS, i


def test_nuclear_sites_geojson():
    path = REPO / "tools" / "map-styles" / "data" / "nuclear-sites.geojson"
    g = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    assert g["type"] == "FeatureCollection"
    feats = g["features"]
    assert len(feats) >= 20
    names: set[str] = set()
    for f in feats:
        assert f["type"] == "Feature"
        assert f["geometry"]["type"] == "Point"
        lon, lat = f["geometry"]["coordinates"]
        pr = f["properties"]
        assert -8.7 <= lon <= 2.0 and 49.8 <= lat <= 60.9, pr["name"]
        assert pr["type"] in {"power-station", "reprocessing", "fuel", "weapons", "naval", "research"}, pr["name"]
        assert pr["status"] in {"operating", "construction", "defuelling", "decommissioning"}, pr["name"]
        assert isinstance(pr["note"], str) and pr["note"], pr["name"]
        assert pr["name"] not in names, pr["name"]
        names.add(pr["name"])
    must = {
        "Sellafield", "AWE Aldermaston", "AWE Burghfield", "HMNB Clyde (Faslane)", "RNAD Coulport",
        "HMNB Devonport", "Barrow-in-Furness shipyard", "Rosyth dockyard", "Dounreay", "Harwell",
        "Winfrith", "Springfields", "Capenhurst", "Hinkley Point C", "Sizewell B", "Heysham 1",
        "Heysham 2", "Hartlepool", "Torness",
    }
    assert sorted(must - names) == []
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `manifest/extended.json`**

182 items, one per line. The 140 `.stackexchange.com` lines plus Super User, Server Fault, Ask Ubuntu, MathOverflow and Stack Apps are generated from the verified OPDS listing (name, size, updated date); every other line is hand-written. OpenStax sizes are estimates (the CDN blocks non-browser downloads; sizes are hints and `sos sync` records the real value), so those `url` items carry no `sha256`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `tools/map-styles/data/nuclear-sites.geojson`**

Coordinates are `[longitude, latitude]` to three decimals (about 100 m). `type` is one of `power-station reprocessing fuel weapons naval research`; `status` is one of `operating construction defuelling decommissioning`.

- [ ] `status` reflects the position on the review date (Heysham and Hartlepool closure dates move; Hinkley Point C and Sizewell C construction status).
- [ ] After plan 04's first full build, replace every overlay `size_bytes` with the measured file size from `manifest/maps.json`.
- [ ] OpenStax items: open each URL in a browser; if a title has moved, update the URL and record the real `size_bytes`.
- [ ] Extended Stack Exchange list regenerated against the OPDS catalogue on the review date (`sos sync --dry-run --tier extended` reports any renamed ZIM).

### Task 3: The 17 shared modules

**Files:**
- Create: `playbooks/modules/water.md`, `food.md`, `shelter-heat.md`, `medical.md`, `sanitation.md`, `power.md`, `comms.md`, `security-law.md`, `navigation.md`, `community.md`, `mental-health.md`, `radiation.md`, `evacuation.md`, `vehicles-fuel.md`, `tools-repair.md`, `growing-food.md`, `livestock.md`
- Modify: `playbooks/README.md` (append the Conventions section)
- Test: `api/tests/test_playbooks_content.py` (create)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `sos.content.parse_document`, `sos.content.validate_tree`, `sos.manifest.load_manifests`, the manifest ids from Tasks 1 and 2, `python-frontmatter`.
- Produces: module slugs (the `MODULES` list below, in `order` 1 to 17) that scenarios declare in `modules:` and include with `{{module:<slug>}}`; `page:` targets referenced here (`water-disinfection`, `uk-numbers`, `what-still-works`, `pmr446`, `amateur-bands`, `knife-firearms-law`, `foraging-law`, `ticks-adders`, `mains-electricity`, `solar-islanding`, `household-plan`) and `card:` targets are created in Tasks 4 and 5; the validator only passes once those exist, so the per-task gate here is pytest plus a validator run whose only errors are missing scenarios, pages and cards.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `api/tests/test_playbooks_content.py`:

```python
"""Content decisions for playbooks/ beyond what sos validate-playbooks checks (sub-plan 03)."""
import json
import re
from pathlib import Path

import frontmatter
import pytest

from sos.content import parse_document, validate_tree
from sos.manifest import load_manifests

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
PB = REPO / "playbooks"
MANIFEST_DIR = REPO / "manifest"

MODULES = [
    "water", "food", "shelter-heat", "medical", "sanitation", "power", "comms", "security-law",
    "navigation", "community", "mental-health", "radiation", "evacuation", "vehicles-fuel",
    "tools-repair", "growing-food", "livestock",
]
MODULE_HEADINGS = ["## Key facts", "## What to do", "## UK specifics", "## Go deeper"]
CITE = re.compile(r"\]\((?:kiwix|doc):[^)]+\)")
ANY_LINK = re.compile(r"\]\(((?:kiwix|doc|module|card|page|playbook|map):[^)]+)\)")
AS_AT = re.compile(r"^\d{4}-\d{2}(-\d{2})?$")


def overlay_ids() -> list[str]:
    rows = json.loads((MANIFEST_DIR / "overlays.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))["items"]
    return [r["overlay"]["id"] for r in rows]


def load(kind: str, slug: str) -> frontmatter.Post:
    return frontmatter.loads((PB / kind / f"{slug}.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))


def h2(body: str) -> list[str]:
    return [line.rstrip() for line in body.splitlines() if line.startswith("## ")]


def sections(body: str) -> dict[str, str]:
    out: dict[str, str] = {}
    current = None
    for line in body.splitlines():
        if line.startswith("## "):
            current = line[3:].strip()
            out[current] = ""
        elif current is not None:
            out[current] += line + "\n"
    return out


@pytest.mark.parametrize("slug", MODULES)
def test_module_front_matter(slug):
    post = load("modules", slug)
    assert set(post.keys()) == {"id", "title", "icon", "order", "summary", "sources"}
    assert post["id"] == slug
    assert post["order"] == MODULES.index(slug) + 1
    assert 20 <= len(post["summary"]) <= 160
    assert len(post["sources"]) >= 3
    for s in post["sources"]:
        assert "title" in s and ("doc" in s or "kiwix" in s), (slug, s)
        if "as_at" in s:
            assert AS_AT.match(str(s["as_at"])), (slug, s)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("slug", MODULES)
def test_module_headings_citations_and_no_task_lines(slug):
    post = load("modules", slug)
    assert h2(post.content) == MODULE_HEADINGS
    body = sections(post.content)
    for name in ("Key facts", "What to do", "UK specifics"):
        assert len(CITE.findall(body[name])) >= 1, (slug, name)
    assert len(ANY_LINK.findall(body["Go deeper"])) >= 4, slug
    assert "- [ ]" not in post.content, slug
    assert "NOMAD" not in post.content


@pytest.mark.parametrize("slug", MODULES)
def test_module_parses_with_sos_content(slug):
    doc = parse_document(PB / "modules" / f"{slug}.md")
    assert doc.id == slug
    assert doc.kind == "module"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the conventions to `playbooks/README.md`**

```bash
cat >> playbooks/README.md <<'EOF'

## Conventions (sub-plan 03)

- Icons are words from the fixed vocabulary in docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-03-03-content.md ("Icon vocabulary"); unknown names render as a book.
- Modules use exactly these headings: `## Key facts`, `## What to do`, `## UK specifics`, `## Go deeper`, and never contain `- [ ]` lines.
- Cards use exactly these headings: `## When to use`, `## Steps`, `## Warnings`, `## Stop or escalate`, `## Source`. Steps 1 to 3 are at most 70 characters; "When to use" is at most 110 characters (the one-screen rule). Warnings start with `**Warning:**`.
- Scenario checklist items always carry an explicit `{#id}` so that rewording never resets anyone's ticks.
- Every dose, distance, time or law is followed by a citation in brackets: `([NRR 2025, p. 45](doc:nrr-2025#page=45))`, `([Prepare](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/))`.
- Zimit article paths are the crawled URL without scheme (`www.gov.uk/buying-carrying-knives`); Wikipedia paths are the underscored title; Stack Exchange paths are `questions/<id>/<slug>`.
- British English; 999, 111, 105, 0345 988 1188; UK drug names; never "NOMAD".
EOF
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `playbooks/modules/water.md` (complete exemplar)**

```markdown
---
id: water
title: Water
icon: water
order: 1
summary: Storing water before an emergency and finding, filtering and disinfecting it after, with UK products and rules.
sources:
  - title: Prepare, get prepared for emergencies
    kiwix: prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Water Treatment Library (zimgit)
    kiwix: zimgit-water_en/home
    as_at: 2024-08-29
  - title: Priority Services Register
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.thepsr.co.uk/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: UK CMOs' public health advice during a national power outage
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/public-health-advice-from-uk-cmos-during-a-national-power-outage
    as_at: 2025-12-16
  - title: Sodium dichloroisocyanurate (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sodium_dichloroisocyanurate
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Protect and Survive
    doc: protect-and-survive-1980
    as_at: 1980-05
  - title: Nuclear War Survival Skills
    doc: nwss
    as_at: 1987-01
---

## Key facts

- Store **2.5 to 3 litres of drinking water per person per day** and keep at least three days' worth; this is the government's own request to every household ([Prepare](kiwix:prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/)). Four people for three days is 36 litres: six 6-litre supermarket bottles.
- Add washing and cooking water on top: aim for 10 to 15 litres a person a day in total if you have the space (water butt, buckets, a filled bath).
- The **Priority Services Register** is free and gets bottled water delivered to your door when supply fails if you are medically dependent on water, disabled, older, or have a child under five; one registration covers your water company and your electricity and gas networks ([Priority Services Register](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.thepsr.co.uk/)).
- Mains water usually keeps flowing for hours to days into a power cut because pumping stations have backup, but it stops quickly in high-rise flats when the booster pumps lose power ([CMO outage advice](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/public-health-advice-from-uk-cmos-during-a-national-power-outage)).
- Boiling is the surest disinfection: bring water to a **rolling boil for one minute**, then let it cool covered ([Water Treatment Library](kiwix:zimgit-water_en/home)). Boiling kills bacteria, viruses and parasites; it does not remove chemicals, fuel or radioactive particles.

## What to do

1. Fill every clean container the moment a warning arrives: kettle, pans, bottles, the bath (for washing and flushing, not drinking).
2. Ration in this order: drinking, cooking, hand washing, dishes, flushing.
3. When the mains fails, use in this order: stored bottled water; the hot-water cylinder (drain from its bottom tap; drinking quality only if the cylinder is fed straight from the mains, otherwise washing only); the toilet cistern (washing only, never the bowl); rainwater and streams after treatment.
4. Treat any water you did not store yourself:
    - **Boil** for one minute ([Water Treatment Library](kiwix:zimgit-water_en/home)).
    - **NaDCC tablets** (Aquatabs and similar, sold in UK outdoor shops): one 8.5 mg tablet per litre, 17 mg per 2 litres, 67 mg per 20 litres, each giving about 5 mg/L of chlorine; wait 30 minutes; double the dose for cloudy water ([Sodium dichloroisocyanurate](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sodium_dichloroisocyanurate)).
    - **Thin household bleach only**: two drops (0.1 ml) of unscented, unthickened 4.5 to 5% sodium hypochlorite per litre, wait 30 minutes; a faint chlorine smell should remain. The dosing table is on [Water disinfection](page:water-disinfection).
    - **Sunlight (SODIS)**: a clear PET bottle laid in full sun for six hours, two days if cloudy ([Solar water disinfection](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Solar_water_disinfection)).
5. Filter cloudy water through cloth, let it settle, then disinfect. Hollow-fibre or ceramic filters (0.1 to 0.2 micron) remove bacteria and parasites but not viruses, so chlorinate or boil afterwards.
6. Never drink floodwater, water from radiators (corrosion inhibitor), swimming pools, or anything downstream of farms, sewage works or an industrial site.
7. Babies: make up formula with water that has boiled and cooled for no more than 30 minutes, so it is still at least 70 °C.
8. Watch for dehydration, especially in children and older people: [Dehydration and rehydration](card:dehydration).

## UK specifics

- **UK bleach is usually "thick" bleach** with thickeners and perfume; it must not be used to treat water. Only thin unscented bleach or NaDCC tablets are suitable ([Water disinfection](page:water-disinfection)).
- Water companies must provide alternative supplies (bottled-water stations, bowsers) when the mains fails and prioritise people on the Priority Services Register; register before the emergency, not during it ([Priority Services Register](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.thepsr.co.uk/)).
- Standpipes and rota cuts were last used in the 1976 drought; drought orders under the Water Industry Act 1991 let companies restrict use ([Water Industry Act 1991](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1991/56/contents)).
- Private supplies (wells, springs, boreholes) are regulated by the Drinking Water Inspectorate and are the owner's responsibility to test and treat ([DWI private supplies](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.dwi.gov.uk/private-water-supplies/)).
- After fallout, water in pipes, covered tanks and sealed bottles is safe; the 1980 advice was to store at least 14 days of water in the fall-out room ([Protect and Survive, p. 7](doc:protect-and-survive-1980#page=7)); fallout particles in open water settle and filter out, so let it stand, pour off the top and filter ([Nuclear War Survival Skills, p. 49](doc:nwss#page=49)).

## Go deeper

- [Water disinfection table](page:water-disinfection)
- [Dehydration and rehydration card](card:dehydration)
- [Water Treatment Library](kiwix:zimgit-water_en/home)
- [Water purification (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Water_purification)
- [Rainwater harvesting (Wikipedia)](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rainwater_harvesting)
- [Reservoirs and water works on the map](map:?overlay=water)
- [Sanitation module](module:sanitation)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the other 16 modules**

Each module has the same four headings and the same front-matter keys as `water.md`. The blocks below are the specification for each: front matter verbatim, then the facts the body must state with the citation to attach to each (write them as prose and lists in the style of `water.md`), then the Go deeper links. Every module cites at least one manifest item in each of Key facts, What to do and UK specifics.

Must contain: Prepare asks for at least three days of non-perishable food, and this box recommends two weeks (Prepare link); energy needs of about 2,000 kcal a day for women and 2,500 for men (Eatwell Guide link); a store list (tinned beans, fish, tomatoes and fruit; dried pasta, rice, oats, lentils; UHT and powdered milk; oil, salt, sugar, honey, stock cubes; tea and coffee; biscuits; peanut butter; baby formula; pet food; a manual tin opener) rotated first-in first-out; power cut rules: keep doors shut, a fridge holds for about 4 hours, a full freezer 48 hours and a half-full one 24 hours, discard chilled food that has been above 8 °C for more than 4 hours (FSA chill link); cooking without mains: gas hob lit with a match, camping stove only in a ventilated space, barbecue and generator never indoors ([Carbon monoxide card](card:carbon-monoxide)); pressure canning for low-acid foods and the botulism risk of water-bath canning them (USDA link, p. via `home`; [Botulism](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Botulism)); foraging is legal for personal use under the Theft Act 1968 s4(3) (four Fs: fruit, foliage, fungi, flowers) but uprooting needs the landowner's permission (Wildlife and Countryside Act 1981 s13) and roadside, dog-walking and sprayed sites are contaminated (FSA foraging link; [Foraging law](page:foraging-law)); the deadly UK plants: hemlock water dropwort ([Oenanthe crocata](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Oenanthe_crocata)) and death cap ([Amanita phalloides](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Amanita_phalloides)); never eat a fungus you cannot name with certainty and never identify from a photo on this box; rationing 1940 to 1954 as the UK precedent ([Rationing in the United Kingdom](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Rationing_in_the_United_Kingdom)); the NRR food-supply contamination and disruption risks ([NRR 2025, p. 122](doc:nrr-2025#page=122)).
Go deeper: `page:foraging-law`, `module:growing-food`, `module:livestock`, `kiwix:zimgit-food-preparation_en/home`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Food_preservation`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Canning`, `card:dehydration`.

```yaml
sources:
  - title: Pit latrine (Wikipedia)

    as_at: 1980-05
```

Must contain: flush with a bucket of grey water while the sewer works; when it does not, use a bucket lined with a bag (urine separate) or dig a pit latrine at least 30 metres from any well, stream or spring and downhill of them, cover each use with soil or ash (Pit latrine link); the 1980 advice was a bucket with a lid and a second one for refuse ([Protect and Survive, p. 8](doc:protect-and-survive-1980#page=8)); hand washing with soap for 20 seconds; surfaces contaminated by vomit or faeces cleaned with 0.5% chlorine (one part 5% thin bleach to nine parts water) and hands with 0.05% (Sodium hypochlorite link); floodwater is sewage-contaminated, wash everything it touched and discard food it reached (UKHSA flooding link); rubbish: keep food waste sealed, bury or burn paper and food, never burn plastics; rats carry leptospirosis ([Leptospirosis](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Leptospirosis)); stock nappies and menstrual products; septic tanks, cesspools and drainage fields for the long term ([Approved Document H, p. 31](doc:ad-h#page=31)); the dead: confirm death, gloves, cool place, wrap and label the body ([Ship Captain's Medical Guide ch. 12, p. 1](doc:scmg-ch12#page=1)); burial on private land in England and Wales is lawful if the death is registered and the grave is at least 30 metres from any spring or watercourse, 10 metres from a field drain and 50 metres from a well or borehole, with no standing water in the grave, recorded in the property deeds ([After a death](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/after-a-death)); cholera and the Broad Street pump as the reason all this matters ([1854 Broad Street cholera outbreak](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/1854_Broad_Street_cholera_outbreak)).
Go deeper: `module:water`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Composting_toilet`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hand_washing`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Natural_burial`, `doc:scmg-ch05`, `page:water-disinfection`.

#### modules/power.md

```yaml
---
id: power
title: Power
icon: bolt
order: 6
summary: What stops when the grid stops, 105 and your network operator, generators, batteries, solar and staying safe around 230 volts.
sources:
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Prepare, be informed about hazards
    kiwix: prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/be-informed-about-hazards/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: HSE INDG231 Electrical safety and you
    doc: hse-indg231
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Approved Document P
    doc: ad-p
    as_at: 2013-04
  - title: iFixit generator guides
    kiwix: ifixit_en_all/Device/Generator
    as_at: 2025-12-21
  - title: Petroleum (Consolidation) Regulations 2014
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2014/1637/contents
    as_at: 2026-09
---
```
Must contain: the NRR reasonable worst case for national electricity transmission failure is a total loss with restoration over several days and up to seven, and rota disconnection afterwards ([NRR 2025, p. 45](doc:nrr-2025#page=45), [p. 90](doc:nrr-2025#page=90)); call **105** free from any phone in Great Britain to reach your electricity network operator (Northern Ireland: NIE Networks 03457 643 643; Republic of Ireland: ESB Networks 1800 372 999), listed on [UK numbers](page:uk-numbers); the network operators by region (UK Power Networks, National Grid Electricity Distribution, SP Energy Networks, SSEN, Northern Powergrid, Electricity North West, NIE Networks, ESB Networks, Manx Utilities, Jersey Electricity, Guernsey Electricity); the Priority Services Register for medical-equipment users; what stops: gas boilers, Digital Voice landlines, most mobile masts after their battery, cash machines, petrol pumps, lifts, electric gates ([What still works](page:what-still-works), Prepare hazards link); fridge and freezer timings ([Food module](module:food)); 230 V 50 Hz, ring finals on 32 A, 13 A plug fuses, a 30 mA RCD, never work on a live circuit ([HSE INDG231, p. 3](doc:hse-indg231#page=3), [Mains electricity](page:mains-electricity)); generators: outdoors at least 6 metres from doors and windows, never in a garage, never back-fed into house wiring through a "suicide lead", only through a transfer switch fitted by an electrician, which is notifiable work ([Approved Document P, p. 7](doc:ad-p#page=7)); petrol at home is limited to 30 litres without notifying the council (Petroleum Regulations link, detail in [Vehicles and fuel](module:vehicles-fuel)); grid-tied solar shuts down in an outage unless it has a battery and an islanding switch ([Solar islanding](page:solar-islanding)); batteries: a 12.8 V 100 Ah LiFePO4 holds about 1,280 Wh, a car battery via an inverter should not be taken below half, a 20,000 mAh power bank runs a phone for a week; LED lighting only, candles as a last resort; compensation under Ofgem's guaranteed standards is claimed from the network operator after restoration; repair guides for generators (iFixit link).
Go deeper: `page:what-still-works`, `page:mains-electricity`, `page:solar-islanding`, `page:uk-numbers`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2025_Iberian_Peninsula_blackout`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Lithium_iron_phosphate_battery`, `map:?overlay=fuel`.

```yaml
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Civil Contingencies Act 2004
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2004/36/contents
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Selling, buying and carrying knives (GOV.UK)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/buying-carrying-knives
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Self-defence in English law (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Self-defence_in_English_law
    as_at: 2026-02-15
---
```
Must contain: Criminal Justice Act 1988 s139 makes carrying a bladed or pointed article in public an offence without good reason; the only exemption is a folding pocket knife with a cutting edge of 3 inches (7.62 cm) or less that does not lock (s139 link, GOV.UK knives link); s141 and the Offensive Weapons Act 2019 ban possession even at home of zombie knives, knuckledusters and the rest of the prohibited list, and restrict corrosive substances (OWA link); zombie-style knives and machetes were added in September 2024 and ninja swords in August 2025 ([Knife and firearms law](page:knife-firearms-law)); Scotland's equivalent is the Criminal Law (Consolidation) (Scotland) Act 1995 s49 ([s49](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1995/39/section/49)); pepper spray and stun guns are prohibited weapons under the Firearms Act 1968 s5 ([s5](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1968/27/section/5)); crossbows are legal to own from 18 (Crossbows Act 1987) and air weapons need no licence in England and Wales under 12 ft·lb but need one in Scotland ([Air Weapons and Licensing (Scotland) Act 2015](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/asp/2015/10/contents)); shotguns and rifles need certificates ([firearms licensing](kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/guidance/firearms-licensing-police-guidance)); reasonable force: Criminal Law Act 1967 s3 and, for householders facing an intruder, force is unlawful only if grossly disproportionate (s76 link, Wikipedia self-defence link); trespass is civil in England and Wales but aggravated trespass and residing on land with vehicles are crimes ([CJPOA 1994 s68](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1994/33/section/68), [PCSCA 2022 s83](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2022/32/section/83)); Scotland's right of responsible access ([Navigation](module:navigation)); taking goods from a shop or house in a disaster is theft or burglary and the courts treated it harshly after the 2011 riots ([2011 England riots](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2011_England_riots)); the Civil Contingencies Act 2004 lets ministers make emergency regulations that can restrict movement and assembly, and defines Category 1 responders (CCA link); there is no "martial law" in UK law: the armed forces assist civil authorities under police control; Terrorism Act 2000 s58 is why this library carries public-domain survival and medical manuals but no tactical material ([s58](kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2000/11/section/58)); practical security is locks, lighting, a dog, neighbours who know each other, not displaying supplies, and walking away from confrontation.
Go deeper: `page:knife-firearms-law`, `page:foraging-law`, `module:community`, `kiwix:legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1986/64/contents`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Civil_Contingencies_Act_2004`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Trespass_in_English_law`, `doc:ad-q`.

```yaml
summary: Grid references, map and compass without GPS, rights of way and access land, and calling mountain rescue.
sources:
  - title: Ordnance Survey National Grid (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Ordnance_Survey_National_Grid
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Irish grid reference system (Wikipedia)

  - title: Naismith's rule (Wikipedia)

  - title: Land Reform (Scotland) Act 2003 section 1
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/asp/2003/2/section/1
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Countryside and Rights of Way Act 2000 section 2
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2000/37/section/2
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: FM 21-76 Survival
    doc: fm-21-76-survival
    as_at: 1992-06
---
```
Must contain: a grid reference is two letters for the 100 km square then eastings before northings; six figures place you within 100 m, eight within 10 m; the box's map shows the reference for the centre and any tapped point, for example OS headquarters is SU 38726 14830 (OS grid link); Northern Ireland and the Republic use the Irish Grid (Irish grid link) and the Channel Islands use latitude and longitude; magnetic variation in the UK in 2026 is small, roughly 0 to 2 degrees east, so read the map margin and otherwise ignore it for rough work ([Magnetic declination](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Magnetic_declination)); Naismith: 5 km an hour plus one minute for every 10 m of climb (Naismith link); phones find GPS position without any network but cannot pass it to this box over plain HTTP, so use the Organic Maps packs on the map's pack page or read the grid reference off the box; rights of way in England and Wales (footpath, bridleway, restricted byway, byway open to all traffic) and CRoW open access land on mountain, moor, heath, down and registered common (CRoW s2 link); Scotland: the right of responsible access to almost all land under the Land Reform (Scotland) Act 2003 and the Scottish Outdoor Access Code (s1 link); Northern Ireland has very few public rights of way; the footpaths and access-land overlays ([map](map:?overlay=footpaths&overlay=access-land)); emergencies: 999, ask for police then mountain rescue, or coastguard at the coast; the distress signal is six whistle blasts or torch flashes a minute, then a minute's pause ([FM 21-76, p. 134](doc:fm-21-76-survival#page=134)); check the weather and daylight; no tide tables in this box (UKHO data is not open), so treat every beach and estuary as a tidal trap.
Go deeper: `map:?overlay=footpaths&overlay=access-land`, `kiwix:outdoors.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Countryside_and_Rights_of_Way_Act_2000`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Land_Reform_(Scotland)_Act_2003`, `module:evacuation`, `doc:fm-3-05-70-survival`.

Must contain: Category 1 responders (police, fire, ambulance, councils, NHS, Environment Agency) plan through Local Resilience Forums in England and Wales, Regional Resilience Partnerships in Scotland and the Emergency Preparedness Groups in Northern Ireland; the LRF list (LRF link); councils open rest centres; RAYNET and 4x4 response groups are the volunteer layer; a community emergency plan has these sections: contacts, local risks, a skills register, an asset register (generators, chainsaws, 4x4s, halls), people who may need help, a rest centre or warm hub, how information is shared when phones are down (noticeboard, runners, a PMR446 channel), who activates the plan and when it is reviewed (Ready Scotland link); the government expects households to be self-sufficient for the first days and wants more community groups ([Resilience Action Plan, p. 21](doc:resilience-action-plan-2025#page=21)); Storm Darragh in December 2024 showed the value of village hubs in Wales ([Wales Resilience Framework, p. 29](doc:wales-resilience-framework-2025#page=29)); check on neighbours, especially anyone on the Priority Services Register; keep politics and blame out of the group; a paper copy of the plan and of the numbers page in the hall.

Must contain: psychological first aid is look, listen, link: notice who is struggling, listen without pressing for detail, connect them to people and practical help (PFA link; Prepare trauma link); the helplines: Samaritans 116 123 free 24 hours, Shout by texting 85258, NHS urgent mental health help through 111 (option 2 in England), CALM 0800 58 58 58, Childline 0800 1111 ([UK numbers](page:uk-numbers)); routine, sleep, food, limiting news, one useful task a day; children: honest simple answers, keep them with familiar adults, let them play and help; grief and the practicalities of a death ([Medical](module:medical)); distress after four weeks that stops normal life may be PTSD and needs a GP (PTSD link; NHS mental health link); ask directly about suicidal thoughts, remove means, do not leave the person alone; alcohol makes every reaction worse.

#### modules/radiation.md

```yaml
---
id: radiation
title: Radiation
icon: radiation
order: 12
summary: Fallout, shielding, the 7:10 rule, stable iodine, decontamination and radiation sickness, with the UK site and iodine arrangements.
sources:
  - title: Planning Guidance for Response to a Nuclear Detonation (FEMA)
    doc: fema-nuclear-detonation-2022
    as_at: 2022-11
  - title: Nuclear War Survival Skills
    doc: nwss
    as_at: 1987-01
  - title: Stable iodine prophylaxis (NRPB 2001)
    doc: nrpb-stable-iodine
    as_at: 2001-01
  - title: Removing radioactive material from your skin and clothes (UKHSA)
    doc: ukhsa-radiation-decontamination
    as_at: 2023-11-28
  - title: Radiation emergencies, information for the public (UKHSA)
    kiwix: govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/government/publications/radiation-emergencies-information-for-the-public
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Acute radiation syndrome (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Acute_radiation_syndrome
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Potassium iodide (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Potassium_iodide
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: REPPIR 2019
    kiwix: legislation_uk/www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2019/703/contents
    as_at: 2026-09
---
```
Must contain: get inside within minutes, into the middle of a building or a basement, and stay there: "get inside, stay inside, stay tuned" ([FEMA, p. 128](doc:fema-nuclear-detonation-2022#page=128)); protection factors: a house basement about 10 to 20, the middle floors of a large concrete building 100 or more, a car about 2 ([FEMA, p. 126](doc:fema-nuclear-detonation-2022#page=126)); halving thicknesses for fallout gamma of roughly 9 cm of packed earth, 6 cm of concrete, 18 cm of water, 22 cm of wood ([NWSS, p. 19](doc:nwss#page=19)); the 7:10 rule: every sevenfold increase in time cuts the dose rate tenfold, so after 7 hours 10% remains, after two days 1%, after two weeks 0.1% ([FEMA, p. 106](doc:fema-nuclear-detonation-2022#page=106)); stay in for at least 48 hours and expect two weeks in the worst areas; the 1980 UK figure was fourteen days ([Protect and Survive, p. 14](doc:protect-and-survive-1980#page=14)); expedient shelters ([NWSS, p. 38](doc:nwss#page=38)); stable iodine protects only the thyroid and only against radioactive iodine, matters most for children, pregnant women and the under-40s, and is taken only when the authorities say so; the UK uses potassium iodate tablets and the age-banded doses are in the NRPB report ([NRPB, p. 9](doc:nrpb-stable-iodine#page=9)); the WHO equivalents in potassium iodide are 130 mg for adults and children over 12, 65 mg for 3 to 12, 32 mg for 1 month to 3 years, 16 mg for newborns (Potassium iodide link; [WHO EML, p. 25](doc:who-eml-2025#page=25)); around nuclear sites tablets are pre-distributed inside the Detailed Emergency Planning Zone under REPPIR 2019 (REPPIR link) and the sites are on the map ([nuclear sites](map:?overlay=nuclear-sites)); decontamination removes up to 90% of contamination: take off outer clothing, bag it, shower with soap, no conditioner, do not scrub ([UKHSA decontamination, p. 1](doc:ukhsa-radiation-decontamination#page=1), [FEMA, p. 40](doc:fema-nuclear-detonation-2022#page=40)); radiation sickness: time to vomiting is the field triage (under an hour is severe), doses above about 6 Gy are usually fatal without intensive care, 1 to 2 Gy is survivable with nursing (ARS link; [Radiation sickness card](card:radiation-sickness)); sealed food and covered water are safe, milk from grazing animals is the main food risk, as the 1957 Windscale fire showed ([Windscale fire](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Windscale_fire); [NWSS, p. 90](doc:nwss#page=90)); a dirty bomb is a blast with a small radiation problem: move upwind and do not touch debris (UKHSA public link); a Kearny fallout meter can be built from a tin and foil ([NWSS, p. 221](doc:nwss#page=221)); units: grays for absorbed dose, sieverts for risk, UK background about 2.7 mSv a year ([Sievert](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Sievert)).
Go deeper: `card:radiation-sickness`, `doc:nwss#page=38`, `doc:protect-and-survive-1980#page=3`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Nuclear_fallout`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fallout_shelter`, `map:?overlay=nuclear-sites`, `playbook:nuclear-accident`.

#### modules/evacuation.md

```yaml
---
id: evacuation
title: Evacuation
icon: exit
order: 13
summary: Deciding whether to stay or go, the grab bag, shutting the house down, rest centres and travelling when roads and fuel are uncertain.
sources:
  - title: Prepare, get prepared for emergencies
    kiwix: prepare_uk/prepare.campaign.gov.uk/get-prepared-for-emergencies/
    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Advice for disabled people and carers (Prepare)

    as_at: 2026-09
  - title: Planning Guidance for Response to a Nuclear Detonation (FEMA)
    doc: fema-nuclear-detonation-2022

  - title: Bug-out bag (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Bug-out_bag
    as_at: 2026-02-15
---
```
Must contain: staying put is the default in the UK; leave only when the police or an Emergency Alert say so, or the building is unsafe (fire, rising water, structural damage, a gas or chemical plume) (Prepare link); the grab bag: documents (passports, insurance, prescriptions, a list of medicines), two weeks of medicines, a litre of water each, snacks, torch, radio, power bank, cash in small notes, keys, first-aid kit, warm layer, waterproof, whistle, pen and paper, baby and pet items, and the card with this box's WiFi name and address (Prepare link; Bug-out bag link); before leaving: turn off gas, electricity and water if it is safe, lock up, leave a note saying where you went, tell someone; rest centres are run by councils with the Red Cross; register on arrival and keep the family together; people with disabilities should have a personal plan and be on the Priority Services Register (Prepare disabled link); transport: keep the tank half full, motorways jam, bicycles get through, never drive or walk through floodwater (15 cm of moving water knocks an adult over, 30 cm floats a car, 60 cm carries it away) (help during flood link); after a nuclear detonation, shelter first and evacuate only when told and along the route given ([FEMA, p. 33](doc:fema-nuclear-detonation-2022#page=33)); chemical plume: go in, stay in, tune in unless told to leave; fire: get out, stay out, call 999; pets come with you, livestock are covered in [Livestock](module:livestock).
Go deeper: `page:household-plan`, `module:navigation`, `module:vehicles-fuel`, `kiwix:govuk_resilience/www.gov.uk/help-during-flood`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Emergency_management`, `map:?overlay=rail&overlay=fuel`.

```yaml

id: vehicles-fuel
```

Must contain: the Petroleum (Consolidation) Regulations 2014 let a household keep up to 30 litres of petrol without telling anyone, in plastic containers of up to 10 litres, metal containers of up to 20 litres or a demountable tank of up to 30 litres; between 30 and 275 litres you must notify the Petroleum Enforcement Authority in writing; diesel is not covered (regulations link); store fuel outside the house in a ventilated place away from ignition ([HSE INDG370, p. 1](doc:hse-indg370#page=1)); petrol goes off in about six months (E10 sooner), diesel keeps a year or more but grows "diesel bug" if it holds water; keep every tank above half; pumps need electricity, as September 2021 showed ([2021 fuel crisis](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/2021_United_Kingdom_fuel_supply_crisis)); siphoning someone else's fuel is theft; an electric car with vehicle-to-load can run a fridge and lights for days from its battery (typically up to 3.6 kW) but cannot be recharged without the grid ([Vehicle-to-grid](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Vehicle-to-grid)); never drive through floodwater, 30 cm floats a car (flood link); winter kit: blanket, shovel, grit, torch, food, water, charger, snow socks; bicycles and hand carts are the fallback ([Bicycles Q&A](kiwix:bicycles.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions)); repairs (mechanics link, [iFixit](kiwix:ifixit_en_all/home/home)).

#### modules/growing-food.md

```yaml
---
id: growing-food
title: Growing food
icon: seedling
order: 16
summary: What grows in the UK climate, when to sow it, how much ground feeds a person, and keeping soil fertile without bought fertiliser.
sources:
  - title: Gardening and Landscaping Q&A
    kiwix: gardening.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions
    as_at: 2026-08-01
  - title: Crop rotation (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Crop_rotation
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Hungry gap (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Hungry_gap
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: Allotment (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Allotment_(gardening)
    as_at: 2026-02-15
  - title: National Risk Register 2025
    doc: nrr-2025
    as_at: 2025-01-16
  - title: Seed saving (Wikipedia)
    kiwix: wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Seed_saving
    as_at: 2026-02-15
---
```
Must contain: the UK calendar: last frost mid-May in the south and early June in the north, first frost October; chit seed potatoes from February, plant first earlies late March to April and lift in June and July, maincrop in April for September; broad beans in November or February, peas March to June, onion sets in March, garlic October to November, carrots April to July, kale and leeks sown April to May for winter; the hungry gap of April and May when stores run out and nothing new is ready (Hungry gap link); a traditional 10-rod allotment (250 m²) was sized to feed a family's vegetable needs (Allotment link); potatoes give about 2 to 3 kg per square metre; three- or four-bed rotation to break disease (Crop rotation link); fertility without fertiliser: compost, manure, urine diluted one to ten, wood ash, comfrey, nitrogen-fixing beans and green manures; save open-pollinated seed, not F1 (Seed saving link); slugs, pigeons and rabbits; cloches, cold frames and a polytunnel extend the season ([Greenhouse](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Greenhouse)); indoors: sprouted seeds and mushrooms ([Fungiculture](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Fungiculture)); the coast: edible seaweed ([Edible seaweed](kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Edible_seaweed)); why: the NRR's food-supply risks ([NRR 2025, p. 122](doc:nrr-2025#page=122)); questions answered in the gardening Q&A (gardening link).
Go deeper: `kiwix:gardening.stackexchange.com_en_all/questions`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Potato`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Dig_for_Victory`, `kiwix:wikipedia_en_all_maxi/Cold_frame`, `module:livestock`, `module:food`, `kiwix:usda-2015_en/home`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Append the failing tests**

```python
CARDS = [
    "cpr-adult", "cpr-child", "severe-bleeding", "choking", "burns", "hypothermia", "heat-stroke",
    "broken-bones", "radiation-sickness", "chemical-exposure", "childbirth", "dehydration",
    "wound-cleaning", "shock", "drowning", "seizures", "anaphylaxis", "carbon-monoxide", "stroke",
    "heart-attack", "recovery-position", "low-blood-sugar", "asthma-attack",
]
CARD_HEADINGS = ["## When to use", "## Steps", "## Warnings", "## Stop or escalate", "## Source"]
STEP = re.compile(r"^\d+\. (.*)$")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("slug", CARDS)
def test_card_front_matter(slug):
    post = load("cards", slug)
    assert set(post.keys()) == {"id", "title", "icon", "order", "summary"}
    assert post["id"] == slug
    assert post["order"] == CARDS.index(slug) + 1
    assert len(post["title"]) <= 40
    assert parse_document(PB / "cards" / f"{slug}.md").kind == "card"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("slug", CARDS)
def test_card_structure_and_screen_rule(slug):
    post = load("cards", slug)
    assert h2(post.content) == CARD_HEADINGS
    body = sections(post.content)
    when = body["When to use"].strip()
    assert 0 < len(when) <= 110, (slug, len(when))
    steps = [STEP.match(l).group(1) for l in body["Steps"].splitlines() if STEP.match(l)]
    assert len(steps) >= 4, slug
    for s in steps[:3]:
        assert len(s) <= 70, (slug, s)
    warnings = [l for l in body["Warnings"].splitlines() if l.strip()]
    assert warnings and all(l.startswith("**Warning:**") for l in warnings), slug
    assert "999" in body["Stop or escalate"], slug
    assert len(CITE.findall(body["Source"])) >= 1, slug
    assert "NOMAD" not in post.content
```
